F*F not conjugate (linear convolution). Values real up to small imag; irfft returns real. Good.

Function `_corr_weights_f(w, f)`: Need h_j = ∑_{r=0}^{n-1} w_{j+r} f_r for j=0..n-1, w length 2n-1. Use FFT. Let fr = f[::-1]. conv = fr * w (linear). Convolution index t: conv[t]=∑_a fr[a] w[t-a]. Need j: a=n-1-r -> t-a = n-1+j - (n-1-r)=j+r. So h[j]=conv[n-1+j]. Full length (n-1)+(2n-2)+1=3n-2. size = next pow2 >=3n-2.
```
fr = f[::-1]
size=1 << ((3*n - 3).bit_length()? length=3n-3? Wait lengths n and 2n-1 => full len n+(2n-1)-1=3n-2. For n=1 length1. Use m=3*n-2, size=1 << (m-1).bit_length().
F= rfft(fr,size), W=rfft(w,size), c=irfft(F*W,size); return c[n-1:n-1+n]
```
Conjugate? fr real, no conjugate, gives linear convolution. Good.

Interpolating original to other n:
```
def _resample(f, n_new):
    n=len(f); x_old=np.linspace(0,1,n); x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n_new)
    g=np.interp(x_new, x_old, f, left=0, right=0)
    g=np.maximum(g,0); s=g.sum(); if s>0 g/=s
    return g
```
This imposes periodic-like endpoints. Could also use `np.interp` with zero padding cyclic? Since sequence positions 0..n-1 not periodic; resampling with endpoints maybe okay. Or use FFT upsample: zero-pad in frequency? Not straightforward.

Another promising method: Use "alternating minimization on top lag pairs". For a top lag k, q_k = ∑ a_i a_{k-i}. If we fix all except a_i and a_{k-i}, to reduce q_k while sum fixed, we can redistribute between symmetric pair: For pair (i,j=k-i), with s=a_i+a_j constant, q contribution a_i a_j = (s^2 - (a_i-a_j)^2)/4, maximized when equal. To reduce contribution, make pair unequal! This is key. The top lag often has many pairs near center; moving mass to make pairs unbalanced can reduce central q. For each active lag k, we can skew mass between i and k-i away from equal. This is a natural local move: choose i<j with sum k, move Δ from larger (?) to one side to reduce product. Since product decreases if difference increases; moving mass from smaller to larger (or vice versa) increases difference and reduces product (for fixed sum). But moving mass changes sum total unless compensated elsewhere? If only swap within pair i,j, total sum of those two constant, overall sum fixed. So we can reallocate between coordinates that contribute to same lag k to reduce q_k, but may affect other lags. This is like "unbalancing".

For a pair (i,j), moving δ from i to j changes q_k for k=i+j by -2δ(a_i+a_j)+δ^2? Specifically pair contribution a_i a_j; if s fixed, product decreases by δ(s-2δ). This can be large for central pairs. More generally, for all lags, moving mass changes convolution as before; choosing j=i? No. Our pair donor/receiver moves are general. But an explicit heuristic: For each top lag k, for each pair (i,k-i), if their values are unequal, move a small fraction from the smaller to larger to increase imbalance (or from larger to smaller? product decreases when difference increases; if a_i<a_j, moving from i to j increases j decreases i, difference increases if? Let s, a_i=x, a_j=s-x. Product x(s-x). If x<s/2 and dx>0, product decreases? derivative s-2x >0, so increasing x (moving from j to i) would increase product if x<s/2; moving from i to j (dx negative) decreases product. So move from smaller to larger? If x<s/2, smaller x, moving from i to j: dx=-δ, product decreases. Yes move from smaller to larger, making larger larger and smaller smaller. This is anti-equalization. That may reduce top lags significantly. Previous coordinate donors high H to low H might do similar if high H corresponds to larger coordinate in top lags.

- For top lags (maybe top 20), for pairs i< j=k-i, compute product contribution and potential reduction if move from smaller to larger fraction p (e.g., 10% of smaller). Evaluate overall max via update. Choose best.

Let's draft code structure:

```
import numpy as np
import time

def _normalize(f):
   f=np.asarray(f,dtype=np.float64)
   f=np.where(f>0, f, 0.0)
   s=f.sum()
   if s < 1e-300: return np.ones(len(f))/len(f)
   return f/s

def _conv_fft(f):
   n=f.size
   m=2*n-1
   if m <=1: return f*f
   size=1 << (m-1).bit_length()
   F=np.fft.rfft(f,size)
   r=np.fft.irfft(F*F,size)[:m]
   # remove tiny negatives
   # r = np.maximum(r,0.0) maybe
   return r

def _corr_top_k(f, c, m, beta, peak):
   # returns gradient array 2*h length n
   L=2*n-1
   if m >= L: idx=np.arange(L); wk=np.ones(L)
   else:
      idx=np.argpartition(c, -m)[-m:]
      val=c[idx]
      if beta <=1 and True: # use weighted by c maybe
         # weights exp((val-peak)/(peak/beta))
         tau=max(peak/beta, 1e-300)
         wk=np.exp((val - peak)/tau)
         s=wk.sum()
         if s < 1e-300: wk=np.ones_like(val); s=L
         wk/=s
      # For direct/FFT: need full w if FFT.
   grad=np.zeros(n)
   nnz=len(idx)
   if nnz <= 40:
      for idx, w in zip(idx, wk):
         lo=max(0, idx-n+1); hi=min(n, idx+1)
         a=idx-hi+1; b=idx-lo+1
         # segment f[a:b][::-1]
         grad[lo:hi] += w * f[a:b][::-1]
   else:
      wfull=np.zeros(L)
      wfull[idx]=wk
      # corr via FFT
      fr=f[::-1]
      size=1 << (3*n - 2 -1).bit_length()? if n=1? handle.
      F=np.fft.rfft(fr, size)
      W=np.fft.rfft(wfull, size)
      conv=np.fft.irfft(F*W, size)
      grad=conv[n-1:n-1+n]
   return 2.0*grad
```
Check corr size: full length len(fr)+len(wfull)-1 = n + (2n-1) -1 =3n-2. For n=1 length1. Need size = 1 << ((3*n - 2) - 1).bit_length()? For m=3n-2. If m=1, bit_length? Use helper `_next_pow2(m)`:
```
def _nxt2(m):
    if m <=1: return 1
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
```
Then `_conv_fft` size _nxt2(2*n-1); `_corr` size _nxt2(3*n-2).

In direct corr, using reversed view `f[a:b][::-1]` with `grad[lo:hi] += w * f[a:b][::-1]` may fail if `lo:hi` length equals b-a? yes. If a=b? length0 no. Good.

But for idx where k > n-1, a=idx-hi+1. Example n=5 idx=7, lo=3, hi=5, a=7-5+1=3,b=7-3+1=5, segment f[3:5][::-1] = [f4,f3], lo:hi [3:5] j=3,4, need f[7-3]=f4, f[7-4]=f3. good.

Weights can sum to1. If beta small, weights near uniform among top m. Could also include rank-based weights: `wk = 1.0` for top m. Fine.

Potential performance: `np.argpartition(c, -m)` modifies? returns indices. If m maybe > L, handle. If `m` > L/2? argpartition on large k? Use if m < L. For m maybe 500 L=12241 good.

`_optimize_mirror` should use `_corr_top_k`. It needs start_time, maybe `initial_peak`.

Pseudo:
```
def _optimize_mirror(f0, deadline, rng, n=None):
    if n is None: n=len(f0)
    f = _normalize(f0)
    x = np.log(np.maximum(f, 1e-300)); x -= x.max()
    f_cls = np.exp(x); f_cls /= f_cls.sum()
    c = _conv_fft(f_cls)
    peak = float(c.max())
    best_peak = peak; best_f = f_cls.copy()
    start = time.monotonic(); total = deadline - start
    if total <=0: return best_f, 2*n*best_peak
    eta=0.35
    beta=2.0
    # top m fraction maybe 0.02
    m = max(32, min(500, int(n*0.02)))
    T = peak*0.02
    mom = np.zeros(n)
    last_imp = time.monotonic()
    accept=0; reject=0; it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # update best from c
       if peak < best_peak - 1e-15:
          best_peak=peak; best_f=f_cls.copy(); last_imp=time.monotonic()
       elapsed=time.monotonic()-start
       progress=min(1.0, elapsed/max(total,1e-6))
       stuck=time.monotonic()-last_imp
       if stuck > 15.0:
           # perturb
           mag = min(0.15, 0.01 + 0.002*(stuck/30.0))
           f_cls = f_cls + (mag/np.sqrt(n))*rng.standard_normal(n)
           f_cls = np.maximum(f_cls,0.0); s=f_cls.sum(); if s>0 f_cls/=s
           x=np.log(np.maximum(f_cls,1e-300)); x-=x.max()
           c=_conv_fft(f_cls); peak=float(c.max())
           mom.fill(0); eta=max(eta,0.3); T=peak*0.1
           continue
       beta_eff = 1.0 + 40.0*progress
       if stuck > 5.0: beta_eff *= 0.3
       grad=_corr_top_k(f_cls, c, m, beta_eff, peak)
       g=grad - grad.mean()
       scal=np.max(np.abs(g)); if scal<1e-30: break
       g/=scal
       mom = 0.9*mom + 0.1*g
       mg=mom - mom.mean(); mscal=np.max(np.abs(mg)); if mscal<1e-30: mg=g; mscal=scal
       mg/=mscal
       x_new = x - eta*mg
       x_new -= x_new.mean()
       # clip
       x_new = np.clip(x_new, -30.0, 30.0)
       f_new = np.exp(x_new); s=f_new.sum(); if s<... continue
       f_new /= s
       c_new = _conv_fft(f_new); peak_new=float(c_new.max())
       delta=peak_new-peak
       if delta <= 1e-14:
          x=x_new; f_cls=f_new; c=c_new; peak=peak_new; eta=min(eta*1.03,1.5); accept+=1
       else:
          # SA acceptance
          acc=False
          if T > 1e-30:
             if np.log(max(rng.random(),1e-300)) < -delta/T:
                acc=True
          if acc:
             x=x_new; f_cls=f_new; c=c_new; peak=peak_new; eta=min(eta*1.01,1.5)
          else:
             eta *= 0.85; reject+=1
             if reject > 200:
                eta=max(0.05, eta*0.5); reject=0
       it +=1
       if it % 2000 == 0:
          print(...)
       T = max(T*0.9995, peak*1e-12)
    # final ensure
    c=_conv_fft(best_f); return best_f, 2*n*float(c.max())
```

```
x_new = np.clip(x_new - x_new.max(), 0? no min)
f_new=np.exp(x_new - x_new.max())
f_new/=sum
# For next x, set x = np.log(f_new) - np.log(...).max()? Rather than keep x large, we can update x to log(f_new) - mean to avoid drift.
x = np.log(np.maximum(f_new, 1e-300)) - np.log(np.maximum(f_new,1e-300)).mean()
```

Now, polish function with exact c updates. Need use current c? In `optimize_mirror`, we have c at end. But for separate polish, we can recompute.

`_polish_pair(f0, deadline, rng)`:
```
def _polish_pair(f0, deadline, rng):
    n=len(f0); f=_normalize(f0); c=_conv_fft(f); cur_peak=c.max(); best_peak=cur_peak; best_f=f.copy()
    accepted=0; no_improve=0; start=time.monotonic(); total=deadline-start
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        # refresh maybe
        if accepted >0 and accepted%200==0:
            # recompute to correct
            f = f/f.sum()
            c = _conv_fft(f); cur_peak=c.max()
        else:
            cur_peak=c.max()
        if cur_peak < best_peak - 1e-15:
            best_peak=cur_peak; best_f=f.copy(); no_improve=0
        # stuck perturb if no_improve > 500?
        # generate candidates
        cand = _candidate_pairs(f,c,n)
        # try candidates
        best_trial_peak=cur_peak; best_move=None
        evaluated=0
        for i,j,frac_hint in cand:
            if i==j: continue
            fa=f[i]; if fa <= 1e-18: continue
            for frac in [0.5,0.2,0.05,0.01,0.002]:
                delta=frac*fa
                if delta <= 1e-18: continue
                # if j receive, ensure f[j]+delta okay no upper bound
                # Quick skip if c[2*i] or c[2*j] near cur_peak and frac large? optional
                tmp = c.copy()
                tmp[j:j+n] += 2.0*delta*f
                tmp[i:i+n] -= 2.0*delta*f
                tmp[2*i] += delta*delta
                tmp[2*j] += delta*delta
                tmp[i+j] -= 2.0*delta*delta
                pk = float(tmp.max())
                if pk < best_trial_peak - 1e-15:
                    best_trial_peak=pk; best_move=(i,j,delta)
                    # break fracs? Since fractions descending, first best maybe; continue for better smaller? We can break if pk very low? Keep all.
                evaluated +=1
            if evaluated > 300: break
        if best_move is not None:
            i,j,delta=best_move
            f[i]-=delta; f[j]+=delta
            c = c.copy()? We need update c exact with chosen move. Instead of recomputing tmp, do update:
            c[j:j+n] += 2.0*delta*f? Careful: f has not changed yet; if we update f first, f is new. Use original f for update. Store f_orig? We have f unchanged if we update c first then f. Do:
                c[j:j+n] += 2*delta*f
                c[i:i+n] -= 2*delta*f
                c[2*i] += delta*delta; c[2*j]+=delta*delta; c[i+j]-=2*delta*delta
                f[i]-=delta; f[j]+=delta
            accepted+=1
            if accepted%200==0: normalize and recompute
        else:
            no_improve+=1
            # random pairs
            improved=False
            for _ in range(20):
                i=int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n)); if i==j continue
                frac = rng.choice([0.05,0.1,0.2])
                delta=frac*f[i]
                if delta<1e-18: continue
                tmp = c.copy(); update; pk=tmp.max()
                if pk < cur_peak - 1e-15:
                    apply; improved=True; break
            if improved:
                no_improve=0
            else:
                if no_improve % 200 == 0:
                    # small perturbation
                    mag=0.005
                    f = f + mag/np.sqrt(n)*rng.standard_normal(n)
                    f=np.maximum(f,0); f/=f.sum(); c=_conv_fft(f); cur_peak=c.max(); no_improve=0
        if no_improve > 3000 and time remaining maybe break?
    return best_f, 2*n*best_peak
```

Candidate generation `_candidate_pairs(f,c,n, rng)`:
Need return list maybe up to 150 tuples (i,j). Use Python set for uniqueness.
```
def _candidate_pairs(f,c,n,rng):
    cands=set()
    # H top/bottom
    m_h = max(32, min(200, n//20))
    idx = np.argpartition(c, -m_h)[-m_h:]
    # weights c[idx]
    h=np.zeros(n)
    for k in idx:
       lo=...; hi=...; a=...; b=...
       h[lo:hi] += c[k] * f[a:b][::-1]
    # scale? not needed.
    # donors top h, receivers bottom h
    if n>1:
       d_idx = np.argsort(-h)[:min(12,n)]
       r_idx = np.argsort(h)[:min(12,n)]
       for i in d_idx:
          for j in r_idx:
             if i!=j and f[i]>0:
                cands.add((int(i),int(j)))
    # unbalance top product pairs
    m_lags = min(12, len(c))
    top_lags = np.argpartition(c, -m_lags)[-m_lags:]
    for k in top_lags:
       lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n,k+1)
       # only i<j: i indices lo to (k//2?)
       # represent i = arange(lo, hi), j=k-i. Want i<j => 2i<k => i < k/2
       end_i = min(hi, (k//2)+1 if k%2==0? For integer i<j: i < k-i -> 2i<k. So i <= (k-1)//2.
       # Also i valid >=lo.
       end_i = min(hi, (k-1)//2 + 1)  # exclusive
       if end_i <= lo: continue
       iarr = np.arange(lo, end_i, dtype=int)
       jarr = k - iarr
       # ensure j in 0..n-1 yes by lo/hi.
       p = f[iarr]*f[jarr]
       P = min(8, len(iarr))
       top = np.argpartition(p, -P)[-P:]
       for t in top:
           i=int(iarr[t]); j=int(jarr[t])
           if f[i] <= 1e-18 and f[j] <=1e-18: continue
           # unbalance: donor smaller, receiver larger
           if f[i] <= f[j]:
              if f[i] > 1e-18: cands.add((i,j))
              else: cands.add((j,i)) # move from other? If both zero skip
           else:
              if f[j] > 1e-18: cands.add((j,i)); else: cands.add((i,j))
    # random few
    for _ in range(20):
       i=int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n)); if i!=j: cands.add((i,j))
    return list(cands)
```
Need caution: `np.arange(lo, end_i, dtype=int)` allocate length ~3000 per top lag; m_lags 12 -> 36k, okay. `p` length same. `np.argpartition` overhead okay per pass. But if polish pass core, this may dominate. We can reduce m_lags=6, P=4. Use 8.

H top/bottom direct loop over m_h maybe 300 -> 300 slice adds per pass, maybe expensive. Could use FFT correlation? But H computation each polish pass; if many passes, costly. We can reduce m_h=64. Starts function:
```
def _generate_starts(base, rng, n_list):
    starts=[]
    for n in n_list:
       # include base resampled if base is not None
       if base.size>0:
           f = base if base.size==n else _resample(base,n)
           starts.append(f)
       # uniform
       starts.append(np.ones(n))
       # ramp
       x=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
       starts.append(x+1.0); starts.append(n-1-x+1.0)
       # quadratics
       starts.append((x+1)**2); starts.append((n-x)**2)
       # gaussian etc.
    # But many arrays; need ensure nonnegative sum.
    return starts
```
Parameter arrays normalized later by `_score_fft`? `_score` can normalize. But keep arrays maybe sum large; normalize in scoring.

Resample:
```
def _resample(f, n_new):
    n = len(f)
    if n == n_new: return f.copy()
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_new)
    g = np.interp(x_new, x_old, f)
    g = np.maximum(g,0.0)
    s=g.sum()
    if s <=0: g = np.ones(n_new)
    else: g/=s
    return g
```
If f not normalized, interp works.

- Uniform: ones.
- Ramps.
- Polynomials: `1 + a*(x-c)^p`.
- Gaussians: loops centers [0.1,0.25,0.4,0.5,0.6,0.75,0.9], widths [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3], add floor 0.001.
- Two gaussians: centers [0.2,0.8], [0.25,0.75], [0.3,0.7], [0.35,0.65], widths [0.08,0.15,0.25].
- Sine/cos: for m in [1,2,3,5,8], A in [0.2,0.5,0.8,1.0], phase in [0,π/2,π]: f=1+A*np.sin(2πm*x+phase); clip at 0.01.

- Random lowpass: generate random coeffs c_m for m=1..M with decay 1/m^2, f=1+∑ c_m sin/cos, clip.

Let's write `_candidate_pairs` more efficiently:
```
def _candidate_pairs(f, c, n, rng):
    cand_set = set()
    peak = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    # H gradient
    m_h = max(8, min(48, n // 32))
    h = _corr_top_k(f, c, m_h, 8.0, peak)
    h = h - h.mean()
    # donors top positive, receivers bottom negative? h nonnegative but centered.
    d_count = min(8, n)
    r_count = min(8, n)
    if n > 1:
        d_idx = np.argpartition(-h, d_count)[:d_count] if d_count < n else np.arange(n)
        r_idx = np.argpartition(h, r_count)[:r_count] if r_count < n else np.arange(n)
        for a in d_idx:
            i = int(a)
            if f[i] <= 1e-18: continue
            for b in r_idx:
                j = int(b)
                if i != j:
                    cand_set.add((i,j))
    # unbalance
    m_lag = min(8, c.size)
    top_lags = np.argpartition(c, -m_lag)[-m_lag:] if m_lag < c.size else np.arange(c.size)
    for k in top_lags:
       intk=int(k)
       lo=max(0,intk-n+1); hi=min(n,intk+1)
       end_i = min(hi, (intk - 1)//2 + 1)
       if end_i <= lo: continue
       # To avoid allocating huge arange if length > 10000? n maybe 12k, okay.
       iarr = np.arange(lo, end_i, dtype=np.int64)
       jarr = intk - iarr
       # filter both within 0..n-1 (should)
       p = f[iarr] * f[jarr]
       P = min(5, len(iarr))
       if P <=0: continue
       if P >= len(iarr):
           top = np.arange(len(iarr))
       else:
           top = np.argpartition(p, -P)[-P:]
       for t in top:
           i = int(iarr[t]); j=int(jarr[t])
           if i==j: continue
           fi=f[i]; fj=f[j]
           if fi + fj <= 1e-18: continue
           if fi <= fj:
               if fi > 1e-18: cand_set.add((i,j))
               else: cand_set.add((j,i))
           else:
               if fj > 1e-18: cand_set.add((j,i))
               else: cand_set.add((i,j))
    # add random
    for _ in range(16):
       if n <=1: break
       i = int(rng.integers(0, n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n))
       if i != j: cand_set.add((i,j))
    return list(cand_set)
```

```
def _score_fft(f):
    f=np.asarray(f,dtype=np.float64)
    f=np.maximum(f,0.0)
    s=f.sum()
    if s <=0: return np.inf
    f=f/s
    n=f.size
    c=_conv_fft(f)
    pk=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    return 2.0*n*pk
```

Let's write functions in detail.

`_nxt_pow2(m)`:
```
def _nxt_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()
```

`_normalize`:
```
def _normalize(f):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if f.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    f = np.where(np.isfinite(f), f, 0.0)
    f = np.maximum(f, 0.0)
    s = float(f.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(f.size, dtype=np.float64) / f.size
    return f / s
```

`_conv_fft`:
```
def _conv_fft(f):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    n = f.size
    m = 2*n - 1
    if m == 1:
        return np.array([f[0]*f[0]], dtype=np.float64)
    size = _nxt_pow2(m)
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, size)
    c = c[:m]
    # clean
    c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)  # This might alter max if roundoff negative only. But if true convolution has zeros, okay.
    return c
```
Wait, clipping negative to zero can hide small negative but also if due to numerical error for true positive slightly below zero? Max unaffected; but if some true coefficients very small positive, negative roundoff ->0, okay. But if c has negative large due to using F*F? Shouldn't. Clipping may slightly affect gradient? c used for top selection; if a small true coefficient negative due to error, okay. But if all c positive, clipping okay. However, for nonnegative f, true convolution nonnegative; roundoff negative should be tiny. Use `c[np.abs(c) < 1e-15] =0`? Simpler `c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)`.

But in coordinate polish exact c updates should not clip; we maintain c separately. In `_conv_fft` clipping after recompute is okay.

`_corr_top_k`:
```
def _corr_top_k(f, c, m, beta, peak):
    n = f.size
    L = 2*n - 1
    if m <= 0 or peak <= 0:
        return np.zeros(n)
    if m >= L:
        idx = np.arange(L)
        val = c.copy()
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -m)[-m:]
        val = c[idx]
    # weights
    tau = max(float(peak) / max(float(beta), 0.1), 1e-300)
    ex = (val - float(val.max())) / tau  # use max val maybe peak or c.max.
    # To avoid underflow, if tau small, exp okay. If ex < -700 underflow.
    wk = np.exp(ex)
    s = float(wk.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        wk = np.ones_like(wk)
        s = float(wk.sum())
    wk = wk / s
    grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    nnz = len(idx)
    if nnz <= 40:
        for ii in range(nnz):
            k = int(idx[ii])
            w = float(wk[ii])
            if w <= 0.0: continue
            lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
            hi = min(n, k + 1)
            if hi <= lo: continue
            a = k - hi + 1
            b = k - lo + 1
            # b-a == hi-lo
            grad[lo:hi] += w * f[a:b][::-1]
    else:
        wfull = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        wfull[idx] = wk
        fr = f[::-1]
        size = _nxt_pow2(3*n - 2)
        F = np.fft.rfft(fr, size)
        W = np.fft.rfft(wfull, size)
        conv = np.fft.irfft(F * W, size)
        grad = conv[n-1:n-1+n]
        grad = np.where(grad > 0.0, grad, 0.0)
    return 2.0 * grad
```
Using `val.max()` instead of peak (same). If beta small, ex near0, weights near1. If beta large, ex negative. Good.
Potential: `np.argpartition(c, -m)` when m close to L; if m= L-1 okay. If m=0 handled. If c contains -0? max0? okay.

`_score_fft`: as above.

`_resample`:
```
def _resample(f, n_new):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = f.size
    if n_new <=0: return np.ones(1)
    if n == 1:
       return np.full(n_new, f[0])
    if n == n_new:
       return f.copy()
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_new)
    g = np.interp(x_new, x_old, f)
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    s = float(g.sum())
    if s <=0:
        return np.ones(n_new, dtype=np.float64)
    return g/s
```

`_optimize_mirror`: Need pass `quiet`.
Detailed:
```
def _optimize_mirror(f0, deadline, rng, n=None, quiet=True):
    f0 = np.asarray(f0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if f0.size == 0: f0=np.ones(128)
    n = f0.size if n is None else int(n)
    # if n passed different from len? Use len.
    f = _normalize(f0)
    # initial score
    c = _conv_fft(f)
    peak = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    best_peak = peak
    best_f = f.copy()
    start = time.monotonic()
    total = max(1e-6, float(deadline) - start)
    if total <= 0:
        return best_f, 2.0*n*best_peak
    x = np.log(np.maximum(f, 1e-300))
    x = x - x.max()
    # params
    eta = 0.25
    momentum = 0.9
    T = max(peak * 0.02, 1e-12)
    m = max(8, min(300, int(n * 0.04)))
    mom = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    last_imp = start
    accept = 0; reject = 0; it = 0
    logmod = 2000 if quiet else 1000
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if peak < best_peak - 1e-15:
            best_peak = peak; best_f = f.copy(); last_imp = time.monotonic()
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        progress = min(1.0, elapsed / total)
        stuck = time.monotonic() - last_imp
        if stuck > 12.0:
            mag = min(0.2, 0.015 + 0.003 * (stuck / 30.0))
            f = f + (mag / np.sqrt(float(n))) * rng.standard_normal(n)
            f = np.maximum(f, 0.0)
            s = float(f.sum())
            if s <=0:
                f = np.ones(n)
                s = float(n)
            f = f / s
            x = np.log(np.maximum(f, 1e-300)); x = x - x.max()
            c = _conv_fft(f); peak = float(c.max())
            mom.fill(0.0)
            eta = max(eta, 0.25)
            T = max(peak * 0.1, 1e-12)
            continue
        beta = 1.0 + 40.0 * progress
        if stuck > 5.0:
            beta *= 0.3
        grad = _corr_top_k(f, c, m, beta, peak)
        g = grad - grad.mean()
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(g))) if g.size else 0.0
        if scale <= 1e-30:
            break
        g = g / scale
        mom = momentum * mom + (1.0 - momentum) * g
        mg = mom - mom.mean()
        mscale = float(np.max(np.abs(mg))) if mg.size else 0.0
        if mscale <= 1e-30:
            mg = g; mscale = scale
        mg = mg / mscale
        x_trial = x - eta * mg
        x_trial = x_trial - x_trial.max()
        x_trial = np.maximum(x_trial, -30.0)
        f_new = np.exp(x_trial)
        s = float(f_new.sum())
        if s <= 1e-30:
            # reset
            f = np.ones(n); ...
            continue
        f_new = f_new / s
        c_new = _conv_fft(f_new)
        peak_new = float(c_new.max())
        delta = peak_new - peak
        accepted_step = False
        if delta <= 1e-14:
            accepted_step = True
            eta = min(eta * 1.04, 1.2)
        else:
            if T > 0 and np.log(max(float(rng.random()), 1e-300)) < -delta / T:
                accepted_step = True
                eta = min(eta * 1.01, 1.2)
        if accepted_step:
            x = x_trial
            f = f_new
            c = c_new
            peak = peak_new
            accept += 1
            # reset mom? keep
        else:
            reject += 1
            eta *= 0.85
            if reject >= 200:
                eta = max(0.05, eta * 0.5)
                mom.fill(0.0)
                reject = 0
            # after many rejects, maybe perturb? stuck handler will if no improve over time.
        it += 1
        if not quiet and it % logmod == 0:
            print("  mirror iter %d peak=%.10f best=%.10f eta=%.3f beta=%.1f acc=%d rej=%d" % (it, peak, best_peak, eta, beta, accept, reject))
        T = max(T * 0.9995, peak * 1e-12)
    # final check
    c = _conv_fft(best_f)
    pk = float(c.max())
    return best_f, 2.0*n*pk
```
Potential: `x_trial = np.maximum(x_trial, -30.0)` after subtract max means some coords set to e-30. Good.
But after reject, `x` unchanged, `f` unchanged. `mom` has been updated with bad grad; may persist. We reset after 200 rejects. Good.

Now, `_polish_pair` implementation with first improvement:
```
def _polish_pair(f0, deadline, rng, quiet=True):
    f = _normalize(f0)
    n = f.size
    c = _conv_fft(f)
    cur_peak = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    best_peak = cur_peak
    best_f = f.copy()
    tmp = np.empty_like(c)
    buf = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    accepted = 0
    no_improve = 0
    need_cands = True
    cand_list = []
    tol = 1e-15
    fracs = (0.4, 0.15, 0.05, 0.01, 0.003)
    start = time.monotonic()
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        # periodic refresh / best
        if accepted > 0 and (accepted % 200 == 0):
            s = float(f.sum())
            if s <= 0: break
            f = f / s
            c = _conv_fft(f)
            cur_peak = float(c.max())
            no_improve = 0
            need_cands = True
        else:
            cur_peak = float(c.max())
        if cur_peak < best_peak - 1e-15:
            best_peak = cur_peak
            best_f = f.copy()
            no_improve = 0
        if need_cands:
            cand_list = _candidate_pairs(f, c, n, rng)
            need_cands = False
        improved = False
        # try candidate list (limit 80)
        max_eval = 0
        for item in cand_list:
            if len(item) == 3:
                prio,i,j = item
            else:
                i,j = item; prio=0
            if max_eval > 500: break
            if i == j: continue
            fi = float(f[i])
            if fi <= 1e-18: continue
            for frac in fracs:
                delta = frac * fi
                if delta <= 1e-18: continue
                two_delta = 2.0 * delta
                np.multiply(f, two_delta, out=buf)
                np.copyto(tmp, c)
                tmp[j:j+n] += buf
                tmp[i:i+n] -= buf
                d2 = delta * delta
                tmp[2*i] += d2
                tmp[2*j] += d2
                tmp[i+j] -= 2.0 * d2
                pk = float(tmp.max())
                max_eval += 1
                if pk < best_peak_global? Use cur_peak? We want improve over current peak. Use cur_peak - tol.
                if pk < cur_peak - 1e-15:
                    # apply move
                    c[j:j+n] += buf
                    c[i:i+n] -= buf
                    c[2*i] += d2
                    c[2*j] += d2
                    c[i+j] -= 2.0*d2
                    f[i] -= delta
                    f[j] += delta
                    cur_peak = pk
                    accepted += 1
                    improved = True
                    no_improve = 0
                    need_cands = True
                    break
            if improved:
                break
        if not improved:
            # random pairs first improvement
            for _ in range(30):
                if n <= 1: break
                i = int(rng.integers(0,n)); j = int(rng.integers(0,n))
                if i == j: continue
                fi=float(f[i])
                if fi <= 1e-18: continue
                frac = (0.1,0.03,0.005)[int(rng.integers(0,3))]
                delta=frac*fi
                if delta <= 1e-18: continue
                two_delta=2.0*delta
                np.multiply(f, two_delta, out=buf)
                np.copyto(tmp,c)
                tmp[j:j+n] += buf
                tmp[i:i+n] -= buf
                d2=delta*delta
                tmp[2*i] += d2; tmp[2*j] += d2; tmp[i+j] -= 2.0*d2
                pk=float(tmp.max())
                if pk < cur_peak - 1e-15:
                   apply; improved=True; break
            if improved:
                continue
            no_improve += 1
            # if stuck, perturb or revert
            if no_improve > 200:
                # try small perturbation around current; if current worse than best, revert
                if cur_peak > best_peak * (1.0 + 1e-8) + 1e-18:
                    f = best_f.copy()
                    c = _conv_fft(f)
                    cur_peak = float(c.max())
                else:
                    mag = min(0.05, 0.005 + 0.001 * (no_improve / 100.0))
                    f = f + (mag / np.sqrt(float(n))) * rng.standard_normal(n)
                    f = np.maximum(f, 0.0)
                    s=float(f.sum())
                    if s>0: f/=s
                    c = _conv_fft(f)
                    cur_peak = float(c.max())
                accepted = 0
                no_improve = 0
                need_cands = True
        if not quiet and (accepted % 1000 == 0): print...
    c = _conv_fft(best_f)
    return best_f, 2.0*n*float(c.max())
```
Need be careful applying move after evaluating using `buf` computed from old f. In in-place apply, `buf` still old scaled f, good. But we compute `tmp[j:j+n] += buf`, `tmp[i:i+n] -= buf`, then apply `c[...] += buf`. Then update f. Good.
However, if `i` or `j` slices overlap? i and j can be any; the two slice additions to tmp and c are independent memory regions that may overlap if j:n? Since update formula exact regardless of overlap when using original f and adding linear terms to c. But performing `c[j:j+n] += buf` then `c[i:i+n] -= buf` if slices overlap, the second subtraction might modify region already added? The exact c' = c + Δf + Δf. If slices overlap, doing both additions in place with precomputed buf is still correct because both are added to same original c; order doesn't matter as long as buf not modified. If overlap, a location gets +buf and -buf if both slices cover; that's correct because linear contributions add. Using in-place sequential addition with same buf is fine (c[pos] += buf[a], then if pos also in other slice c[pos] -= buf[b]). Good.
Sparse updates: If `2*i == 2*j` (i=j) skip; if `i+j` equals 2i or2j? When i or j zero? If i=0, i+j=j, 2i=0; separate unless j=0. If j=0, 2j=0 and i+j=i; if i>0 separate. If i+j equals 2i when j=i (skip). If i+j equals 2j when i=j. So no duplicate except skip. Good.

But applying `c[j:j+n] += buf` modifies c; then `c[i:i+n] -= buf`. If `i<j`, slices may overlap but buf values differ by offset. In-place is okay as discussed. No need original c for second? It subtracts from updated c, which equals original + first addition; final original + first - second. Fine.

After move, `f[i] -= delta`, `f[j] += delta`. If f had sum1, preserved. If f[i] becomes negative tiny due to delta=0.4 f[i], no.

In no-improve branch, `need_cands` remains False if no perturb; but cands same and all failed exact; next loop will try again wasted unless no_improve triggers perturb. We can set `need_cands = False`, but after no improvement from cands, and random also no improvement, next iteration will call cands again (same) unless no_improve resets. To avoid repeated, set `no_improve +=1` and if no_improve <200, maybe we don't need new cands; but we will loop and try cands again. That's bad. We can add a flag `cands_exhausted = True` to skip candidate evaluation until f changes. Simpler: In no-improved branch, after random fail, set `cand_list = []` so next loop candidate loop does nothing; random fail again; no_improve count increases until perturb. Let's do:

```
   cand_list = []  # skip candidates on subsequent iterations until perturbation
   no_improve += 1
   ... if no_improve >200 perturb (set need_cands True, cand_list new)
```
But if we skip cands every loop, random pairs 30 each loop; after 200 loops, perturb. That's okay. Or break early to perturb after 50 no-improve. Set threshold 50 for polish. Since cands exact exhausted, main hope random/perturb. Use 100.

Implement `_candidate_pairs` with H and unbalance.
```
def _candidate_pairs(f,c,n,rng):
    cand=[]
    if n <=1: return cand
    peak = float(c.max())
    # H
    m_h = max(4, min(32, n // 16))
    h = _corr_top_k(f,c,m_h,8.0,peak)
    h = h - h.mean()
    d_count = min(6, n)
    r_count = min(6, n)
    d_idx = np.argpartition(h, -d_count)[:d_count] if d_count < n else np.arange(n)
    r_idx = np.argpartition(h, r_count)[:r_count] if r_count < n else np.arange(n)
    d_sorted = d_idx[np.argsort(-h[d_idx])]
    r_sorted = r_idx[np.argsort(h[r_idx])]
    for da in d_sorted:
       i=int(da)
       if f[i] <= 1e-18: continue
       for ra in r_sorted:
          j=int(ra)
          if i==j: continue
          prio = float(h[i] - h[j])
          if prio > 1e-18:
             cand.append((prio*1000.0, i, j))
    # unbalance
    L=c.size
    m_lag = min(6, L)
    top_lags = np.argpartition(c, -m_lag)[-m_lag:] if m_lag < L else np.arange(L)
    u_cand=[]
    for kk in top_lags:
       k=int(kk)
       lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n,k+1)
       end_i = min(hi, (k-1)//2 + 1)
       if end_i <= lo: continue
       iarr = np.arange(lo, end_i, dtype=np.int64)
       jarr = k - iarr
       p = f[iarr] * f[jarr]
       P = min(4, len(iarr))
       if P <= 0: continue
       if P >= len(iarr):
          top = np.arange(len(iarr))
       else:
          top = np.argpartition(p, -P)[-P:]
       for t in top:
          i=int(iarr[t]); j=int(jarr[t])
          if i==j: continue
          fi=float(f[i]); fj=float(f[j])
          if fi + fj <= 1e-18: continue
          if fi <= fj:
             if fi > 1e-18:
                u_cand.append((fi, i, j))
             else:
                u_cand.append((fj, j, i))
          else:
             if fj > 1e-18:
                u_cand.append((fj, j, i))
             else:
                u_cand.append((fi, i, j))
    u_cand.sort(reverse=True)
    cand.extend(u_cand[:20])
    # random
    for _ in range(12):
       i=int(rng.integers(0,n)); j=int(rng.integers(0,n))
       if i != j:
          cand.append((0.0, i, j))
    # remove duplicates? to avoid reeval. Use set of pairs.
    seen=set(); uniq=[]; limit_len=80
    for item in cand:
       p1=item[1]; p2=item[2]
       key=(p1,p2)
       if key in seen: continue
       seen.add(key); uniq.append(item)
       if len(uniq) >= limit_len: break
    return uniq
```
Priorities scales: H prio*1000? h diff ~1/n=1e-4; *1000 ~0.1. Unbalance prio fi ~1/n=1e-4. They are comparable. Good. H likely first.

But H candidates from argpartition top 6/bottom 6 may include donor/receiver same; skip. If no positive prio, none.

```
def _starts_for_length(n, rng, count=60):
    out=[]
    x = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    out.append(np.ones(n))
    out.append(i + 1.0)
    out.append(n - i)
    out.append((i + 1.0)**2)
    out.append((n - i)**2)
    # gauss
    centers = [0.2,0.35,0.5,0.65,0.8]
    widths = [0.08,0.15,0.25]
    for c in centers:
      for w in widths:
        out.append(np.exp(-((x-c)/w)**2)+1e-3)
    two centers pairs...
    # trig
    for m in [1,2,4,8]:
       for A in [0.3,0.8,1.2]:
          for ph in [0.0, np.pi/2]:
             out.append(1.0 + A*np.sin(2*np.pi*m*x + ph))
             out.append(1.0 + A*np.cos(2*np.pi*m*x + ph))
    # random lowpass
    while len(out) < count:
       r = rng.random()
       if r < 0.25:
          out.append(rng.exponential(1.0,n)+0.01)
       elif r < 0.5:
          out.append(np.abs(rng.standard_normal(n))+0.01)
       elif r < 0.75:
          out.append(np.ones(n)+0.4*rng.standard_normal(n))
       else:
          out.append(_random_lowpass(n,rng))
    # trim to count maybe
    return out[:count]
```
Need `_random_lowpass` top-level. Or implement inline with array but while loop uses. Define:
```
def _random_lowpass(n,rng):
    f=np.ones(n)
    x=np.arange(n,dtype=np.float64)
    M=min(32,n-1)
    for m in range(1, M+1):
       amp = 0.5 / (m*m)
       a = amp * rng.standard_normal()
       b = amp * rng.standard_normal()
       w = 2*np.pi*m/n
       f = f + a*np.sin(w*x) + b*np.cos(w*x)
    return np.maximum(f,1e-3)
```
No closures. Good.
Clip to positive later in scoring.

Could produce arrays with negative values; scoring normalizes and maximum 0. Good.

```
def _coarse_starts(base, n_list, rng, coarse_deadline):
    starts=[]
    if coarse_deadline <= time.monotonic(): return starts
    n0=base.size
    Ms=[]
    for M in [64,128,256,512]:
       if M < 32: M=32
       if M <= max(32, n0):
          if M not in Ms: Ms.append(M)
    Ms=Ms[-3:]
    if not Ms: Ms=[min(128,max(32,n0))]
    total_runs = len(Ms)*4
    if total_runs <=0: return starts
    per = max(5.0, (coarse_deadline - time.monotonic()) / float(total_runs))
    count=0
    for M in Ms:
       x=np.linspace(0,1,M)
       seed_starts=[np.ones(M), np.arange(M)+1.0, np.exp(-((x-0.25)/0.2)**2)+np.exp(-((x-0.75)/0.2)**2)+1e-3, rng.exponential(1.0,M)+0.01]
       for fseed in seed_starts:
          if time.monotonic() >= coarse_deadline - 5: break
          f0 = _normalize(fseed)
          # avoid huge time if per too large
          run_end = time.monotonic() + min(per, coarse_deadline - time.monotonic() - 2)
          # optimize first 70%
          opt_end = time.monotonic() + 0.7*(run_end - time.monotonic())
          f_opt, sc_opt = _optimize_mirror(f0, opt_end, rng, quiet=True)
          # choose best vs f0? optimize returns best within including initial maybe.
          pol_end = run_end
          if pol_end > time.monotonic() + 5:
             f_pol, sc_pol = _polish_pair(f_opt, pol_end, rng, quiet=True)
             if sc_pol < sc_opt: f_best=f_pol
             else: f_best=f_opt
          else: f_best=f_opt
          # resample to target lengths
          for n in n_list:
             fr = _resample(f_best, n)
             starts.append(fr)
          count+=1
    return starts
```

But actual t didn't progress, so main_end not decreased; total allocated can exceed main_end? At third, remaining_main = main_end - t0 = R, rem_w_after two=1, alloc=R. But we already did first with no time; total time actual 0+0+R=R. It doesn't exceed main_end because first didn't consume time. The allocated concept not equal consumed; if start uses 0, later can use more, total consumed R. If first used half alloc consumed R/3*0.5, next rem R - R/6=5R/6, rem_w_after=2, alloc=(5R/6)*(1/2)=5R/12; second maybe uses all; final rem=5R/6-5R/12=5R/12, last alloc=5R/12 (rem_w=1), total consumed=R. Potential issue: If a start's optimize/polish internally could exceed its `opt_end` due to time resolution? They check while time<deadline, no big overrun.

```python
"""
Multi-scale optimizer for non-negative step functions.

Strategy:
- Work on the scale-invariant objective 2*n*max(conv(f,f))/(sum f)^2
  by normalizing sum(f)=1.
- Use coarse-to-fine continuation: optimize short sequences, resample them,
  and use them as starts for the target lengths.
- Main local optimizer: mirror descent in log-space with a smoothed top-lag
  subgradient (softmax over high convolution coefficients) and simulated
  annealing acceptance.
- Final/periodic coordinate polish: exact rank-2 moves (move mass from one
  coordinate to another) chosen by top-lag gradients and by unbalancing high
  contributing pairs, using O(n) convolution updates.
- Explore several length scales and diverse parametric/random starts.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def _nxt_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _normalize(f):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if f.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    f = np.where(np.isfinite(f), f, 0.0)
    f = np.maximum(f, 0.0)
    s = float(f.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(f.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(f.size)
    return f / s


def _conv_fft(f):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64)
    n = f.size
    m = 2 * n - 1
    if m == 1:
        return np.array([f[0] * f[0]], dtype=np.float64)
    size = _nxt_pow2(m)
    F = np.fft.rfft(f, size)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, size)
    c = c[:m]
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def _score_fft(f):
    f = _normalize(f)
    n = f.size
    c = _conv_fft(f)
    pk = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    return 2.0 * float(n) * pk


def _resample(f, n_new):
    f = np.asarray(f, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = f.size
    if n_new <= 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.full(n_new, f[0], dtype=np.float64)
    if n == n_new:
        return f.copy()
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_new)
    g = np.interp(x_new, x_old, f)
    g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)
    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    s = float(g.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones(n_new, dtype=np.float64)
    return g / s


def _corr_top_k(f, c, m, beta, peak):
    """
    Gradient (up to a positive constant) for a weighted sum of top convolution
    coefficients. For a lag k, d q_k / d f_j = 2 f_{k-j} on the valid interval.
    """
    n = f.size
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if m <= 0 or peak <= 0.0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if m >= L:
        idx = np.arange(L)
        val = c.copy()
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -m)[-m:]
        val = c[idx]

    beta = max(float(beta), 0.1)
    tau = max(float(peak) / beta, 1e-300)
    vmax = float(val.max())
    exp_arg = (val - vmax) / tau
    wk = np.exp(exp_arg)
    s = float(wk.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        wk = np.ones_like(wk, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(wk.sum())
    wk = wk / s

    grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    nnz = len(idx)

    if nnz <= 40:
        for ii in range(nnz):
            k = int(idx[ii])
            w = float(wk[ii])
            if w <= 0.0:
                continue
            lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
            hi = min(n, k + 1)
            if hi <= lo:
                continue
            a = k - hi + 1
            b = k - lo + 1
            grad[lo:hi] += w * f[a:b][::-1]
    else:
        wfull = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        wfull[idx] = wk
        fr = f[::-1]
        size = _nxt_pow2(3 * n - 2)
        F = np.fft.rfft(fr, size)
        W = np.fft.rfft(wfull, size)
        conv = np.fft.irfft(F * W, size)
        grad = conv[n - 1:n - 1 + n]
        grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
        grad = np.maximum(grad, 0.0)

    return 2.0 * grad


def _optimize_mirror(f0, deadline, rng, quiet=True):
    """Mirror descent with smoothed top-lag gradients and SA acceptance."""
    f0 = _normalize(f0)
    n = f0.size
    c = _conv_fft(f0)
    peak = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    best_peak = peak
    best_f = f0.copy()

    start = time.monotonic()
    total = max(1e-6, float(deadline) - start)
    if total <= 0.0:
        return best_f, 2.0 * n * best_peak

    x = np.log(np.maximum(f0, 1e-300))
    x = x - x.max()

    eta = 0.25
    momentum = 0.90
    T = max(peak * 0.02, 1e-12)
    m = max(8, min(300, int(n * 0.04)))
    mom = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    last_imp = start
    accept = 0
    reject = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if peak < best_peak - 1e-15:
            best_peak = peak
            best_f = f0.copy() if f0 is not None else f0
            best_f = f0.copy()
            last_imp = time.monotonic()

        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        progress = min(1.0, elapsed / total)
        stuck = time.monotonic() - last_imp

        if stuck > 12.0:
            mag = min(0.20, 0.015 + 0.003 * (stuck / 30.0))
            f0 = f0 + (mag / np.sqrt(float(n))) * rng.standard_normal(n)
            f0 = np.maximum(f0, 0.0)
            s = float(f0.sum())
            if s <= 1e-300:
                f0 = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
            else:
                f0 = f0 / s
            x = np.log(np.maximum(f0, 1e-300))
            x = x - x.max()
            c = _conv_fft(f0)
            peak = float(c.max())
            mom.fill(0.0)
            eta = max(eta, 0.25)
            T = max(peak * 0.10, 1e-12)
            continue

        beta = 1.0 + 40.0 * progress
        if stuck > 5.0:
            beta *= 0.30

        grad = _corr_top_k(f0, c, m, beta, peak)
        g = grad - float(grad.mean())
        scale = float(np.max(np.abs(g))) if g.size else 0.0
        if scale <= 1e-30:
            break
        g = g / scale

        dirg = momentum * mom + (1.0 - momentum) * g
        mg = dirg - float(dirg.mean())
        mscale = float(np.max(np.abs(mg))) if mg.size else 0.0
        if mscale <= 1e-30:
            mg = g
            mscale = scale
        mg = mg / mscale

        x_trial = x - eta * mg
        x_trial = x_trial - x_trial.max()
        x_trial = np.maximum(x_trial, -30.0)
        f_new = np.exp(x_trial)
        s = float(f_new.sum())
        if s <= 1e-30:
            x = x - x.max()
            f0 = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
            c = _conv_fft(f0)
            peak = float(c.max())
            continue
        f_new = f_new / s

        c_new = _conv_fft(f_new)
        peak_new = float(c_new.max())
        delta = peak_new - peak

        accepted = False
        if delta <= 1e-14:
            accepted = True
            eta = min(eta * 1.04, 1.2)
        else:
            if T > 0.0:
                if np.log(max(float(rng.random()), 1e-300)) < -delta / T:
                    accepted = True
                    eta = min(eta * 1.01, 1.2)

        if accepted:
            mom = dirg
            x = x_trial
            f0 = f_new
            c = c_new
            peak = peak_new
            accept += 1
            if peak_new < best_peak - 1e-15:
                best_peak = peak_new
                best_f = f_new.copy()
                last_imp = time.monotonic()
        else:
            reject += 1
            eta *= 0.85
            if reject >= 200:
                eta = max(0.05, eta * 0.5)
                mom.fill(0.0)
                reject = 0

        it += 1
        if not quiet and it % 2000 == 0:
            print("    mirror it=%d peak=%.10f best=%.10f eta=%.3f beta=%.1f acc=%d rej=%d"
                  % (it, peak, best_peak, eta, beta, accept, reject))

        T = max(T * 0.9995, peak * 1e-12)

    c = _conv_fft(best_f)
    pk = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    return best_f, 2.0 * n * pk


def _candidate_pairs(f, c, n, rng):
    """Generate high-priority rank-2 moves as (priority, donor, receiver)."""
    cand = []
    if n <= 1:
        return cand

    peak = float(c.max())

    # Gradient-based donors/receivers.
    m_h = max(4, min(32, n // 16))
    h = _corr_top_k(f, c, m_h, 8.0, peak)
    h = h - float(h.mean())

    d_count = min(6, n)
    r_count = min(6, n)
    d_idx = np.argpartition(h, -d_count)[:d_count] if d_count < n else np.arange(n)
    r_idx = np.argpartition(h, r_count)[:r_count] if r_count < n else np.arange(n)

    d_sorted = d_idx[np.argsort(-h[d_idx])]
    r_sorted = r_idx[np.argsort(h[r_idx])]

    for da in d_sorted:
        i = int(da)
        fi = float(f[i])
        if fi <= 1e-18:
            continue
        for ra in r_sorted:
            j = int(ra)
            if i == j:
                continue
            prio = float(h[i] - h[j])
            if prio > 1e-18:
                cand.append((prio * 1000.0, i, j))

    # Unbalance high-contributing pairs on top lags.
    L = c.size
    m_lag = min(6, L)
    top_lags = np.argpartition(c, -m_lag)[-m_lag:] if m_lag < L else np.arange(L)
    u_cand = []
    for kk in top_lags:
        k = int(kk)
        lo = max(0, k - n + 1)
        hi = min(n, k + 1)
        end_i = min(hi, (k - 1) // 2 + 1)
        if end_i <= lo:
            continue
        iarr = np.arange(lo, end_i, dtype=np.int64)
        jarr = k - iarr
        p = f[iarr] * f[jarr]
        P = min(4, len(iarr))
        if P <= 0:
            continue
        if P >= len(iarr):
            top = np.arange(len(iarr))
        else:
            top = np.argpartition(p, -P)[-P:]
        for t in top:
            i = int(iarr[t])
            j = int(jarr[t])
            if i == j:
                continue
            fi = float(f[i])
            fj = float(f[j])
            if fi + fj <= 1e-18:
                continue
            if fi <= fj:
                if fi > 1e-18:
                    u_cand.append((fi, i, j))
                else:
                    u_cand.append((fj, j, i))
            else:
                if fj > 1e-18:
                    u_cand.append((fj, j, i))
                else:
                    u_cand.append((fi, i, j))

    u_cand.sort(reverse=True)
    cand.extend(u_cand[:20])

    # Random fallbacks.
    for _ in range(12):
        i = int(rng.integers(0, n))
        j = int(rng.integers(0, n))
        if i != j:
            cand.append((0.0, i, j))

    cand.sort(reverse=True)

    seen = set()
    uniq = []
    for item in cand:
        key = (item[1], item[2])
        if key in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(key)
        uniq.append(key[1:] if False else item)
        if len(uniq) >= 80:
            break
    return uniq


def _polish_pair(f0, deadline, rng, quiet=True):
    """Exact rank-2 coordinate polish with O(n) convolution updates."""
    f = _normalize(f0)
    n = f.size
    if n <= 2:
        return f, 2.0 * n * float(_conv_fft(f).max())

    c = _conv_fft(f)
    cur_peak = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    best_peak = cur_peak
    best_f = f.copy()

    tmp = np.empty_like(c, dtype=np.float64)
    buf = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    fracs = (0.40, 0.15, 0.05, 0.01, 0.003)
    accepted = 0
    no_improve = 0
    need_cands = True
    cand_list = []

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if accepted > 0 and (accepted % 200 == 0):
            s = float(f.sum())
            if s <= 1e-300:
                break
            f = f / s
            c = _conv_fft(f)
            cur_peak = float(c.max())
            accepted = 0
            no_improve = 0
            need_cands = True
        else:
            cur_peak = float(c.max())

        if cur_peak < best_peak - 1e-15:
            best_peak = cur_peak
            best_f = f.copy()
            no_improve = 0

        if need_cands:
            cand_list = _candidate_pairs(f, c, n, rng)
            need_cands = False

        improved = False

        # Try structured candidates until the first improvement is found.
        max_evals = 0
        for item in cand_list:
            if max_evals > 500:
                break
            prio, i, j = item
            if i == j:
                continue
            fi = float(f[i])
            if fi <= 1e-18:
                continue
            for frac in fracs:
                delta = frac * fi
                if delta <= 1e-18:
                    continue
                two_delta = 2.0 * delta
                np.multiply(f, two_delta, out=buf)
                np.copyto(tmp, c)
                tmp[j:j + n] += buf
                tmp[i:i + n] -= buf
                d2 = delta * delta
                tmp[2 * i] += d2
                tmp[2 * j] += d2
                tmp[i + j] -= 2.0 * d2
                pk = float(tmp.max())
                max_evals += 1
                if pk < cur_peak - 1e-15:
                    c[j:j + n] += buf
                    c[i:i + n] -= buf
                    c[2 * i] += d2
                    c[2 * j] += d2
                    c[i + j] -= 2.0 * d2
                    f[i] -= delta
                    f[j] += delta
                    cur_peak = pk
                    accepted += 1
                    improved = True
                    no_improve = 0
                    need_cands = True
                    if cur_peak < best_peak - 1e-15:
                        best_peak = cur_peak
                        best_f = f.copy()
                    break
            if improved:
                break

        if not improved:
            # A few random rank-2 moves.
            for _ in range(30):
                i = int(rng.integers(0, n))
                j = int(rng.integers(0, n))
                if i == j:
                    continue
                fi = float(f[i])
                if fi <= 1e-18:
                    continue
                frac = (0.10, 0.03, 0.005)[int(rng.integers(0, 3))]
                delta = frac * fi
                if delta <= 1e-18:
                    continue
                two_delta = 2.0 * delta
                np.multiply(f, two_delta, out=buf)
                np.copyto(tmp, c)
                tmp[j:j + n] += buf
                tmp[i:i + n] -= buf
                d2 = delta * delta
                tmp[2 * i] += d2
                tmp[2 * j] += d2
                tmp[i + j] -= 2.0 * d2
                pk = float(tmp.max())
                if pk < cur_peak - 1e-15:
                    c[j:j + n] += buf
                    c[i:i + n] -= buf
                    c[2 * i] += d2
                    c[2 * j] += d2
                    c[i + j] -= 2.0 * d2
                    f[i] -= delta
                    f[j] += delta
                    cur_peak = pk
                    accepted += 1
                    improved = True
                    no_improve = 0
                    need_cands = True
                    if cur_peak < best_peak - 1e-15:
                        best_peak = cur_peak
                        best_f = f.copy()
                    break

            if improved:
                continue

            # Candidates are exhausted; skip them until the state changes.
            cand_list = []
            no_improve += 1

            if no_improve > 80:
                # If wandering above the best, revert; otherwise perturb.
                if cur_peak > best_peak * (1.0 + 1e-8) + 1e-18:
                    f = best_f.copy()
                    c = _conv_fft(f)
                    cur_peak = float(c.max())
                else:
                    mag = min(0.05, 0.005 + 0.001 * (no_improve / 40.0))
                    f = f + (mag / np.sqrt(float(n))) * rng.standard_normal(n)
                    f = np.maximum(f, 0.0)
                    s = float(f.sum())
                    if s > 1e-300:
                        f = f / s
                    c = _conv_fft(f)
                    cur_peak = float(c.max())
                accepted = 0
                no_improve = 0
                need_cands = True
                cand_list = []

        if not quiet and (accepted % 2000 == 0):
            print("    polish accepted=%d current=%.10f best=%.10f" % (accepted, cur_peak, best_peak))

    c = _conv_fft(best_f)
    pk = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    return best_f, 2.0 * n * pk


def _random_lowpass(n, rng):
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    f = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    M = min(32, max(1, n - 1))
    for m in range(1, M + 1):
        amp = 0.5 / float(m * m)
        a = amp * rng.standard_normal()
        b = amp * rng.standard_normal()
        w = 2.0 * np.pi * float(m) / float(n)
        f = f + a * np.sin(w * x) + b * np.cos(w * x)
    return np.maximum(f, 1e-3)


def _starts_for_length(n, rng, count=60):
    out = []
    if n <= 0:
        return out

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)

    # Basic shapes.
    out.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    out.append(i + 1.0)
    out.append(n - i)
    out.append((i + 1.0) ** 2)
    out.append((n - i) ** 2)

    # Single Gaussians.
    for c in (0.20, 0.35, 0.50, 0.65, 0.80):
        for w in (0.08, 0.15, 0.25):
            out.append(np.exp(-((x - c) / w) ** 2) + 1e-3)

    # Two Gaussians.
    for c1, c2 in ((0.20, 0.80), (0.25, 0.75), (0.30, 0.70), (0.10, 0.90), (0.35, 0.65)):
        for w in (0.08, 0.15, 0.25):
            out.append(np.exp(-((x - c1) / w) ** 2) + np.exp(-((x - c2) / w) ** 2) + 1e-3)

    # Low-frequency trigonometric shapes.
    for m in (1, 2, 4, 8):
        for A in (0.3, 0.8, 1.2):
            for ph in (0.0, 0.5 * np.pi):
                out.append(1.0 + A * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * float(m) * x + ph))
                out.append(1.0 + A * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * float(m) * x + ph))

    # High-frequency aliasing / unbalancing shapes.
    for period in (2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64):
        for A in (0.3, 0.6, 0.9):
            out.append(1.0 + A * np.cos(np.pi * i / float(period)))
            out.append(1.0 + A * np.sin(np.pi * i / float(period)))

    # Random starts.
    while len(out) < count:
        r = rng.random()
        if r < 0.25:
            out.append(rng.exponential(1.0, n) + 0.01)
        elif r < 0.50:
            out.append(np.abs(rng.standard_normal(n)) + 0.01)
        elif r < 0.75:
            out.append(np.ones(n) + 0.4 * rng.standard_normal(n))
        else:
            out.append(_random_lowpass(n, rng))

    if count > 0 and len(out) > count:
        return out[:count]
    return out


def _coarse_starts(base, n_list, rng, coarse_deadline):
    """Optimize multiple short sequences and resample them to target lengths."""
    starts = []
    if coarse_deadline <= time.monotonic():
        return starts

    n0 = base.size
    Ms = []
    for M in (64, 128, 256, 512):
        if M < 32:
            M = 32
        if M <= max(32, n0):
            if M not in Ms:
                Ms.append(M)
    Ms = Ms[-3:]
    if not Ms:
        Ms = [min(128, max(32, n0))]

    seeds_per_M = 4
    total_runs = len(Ms) * seeds_per_M
    if total_runs <= 0:
        return starts

    per = max(5.0, (coarse_deadline - time.monotonic()) / float(total_runs))

    done = 0
    for M in Ms:
        if time.monotonic() >= coarse_deadline - 5.0:
            break
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, M)
        seed_starts = [
            np.ones(M, dtype=np.float64),
            np.arange(M, dtype=np.float64) + 1.0,
            np.exp(-((x - 0.25) / 0.20) ** 2) + np.exp(-((x - 0.75) / 0.20) ** 2) + 1e-3,
            rng.exponential(1.0, M) + 0.01,
        ]
        if base.size > 0:
            seed_starts.append(_resample(base, M))

        for fseed in seed_starts[:seeds_per_M]:
            if time.monotonic() >= coarse_deadline - 5.0:
                break
            f0 = _normalize(fseed)
            run_end = min(coarse_deadline - 2.0, time.monotonic() + per)
            if run_end <= time.monotonic() + 2.0:
                break
            opt_end = time.monotonic() + 0.70 * (run_end - time.monotonic())
            f_opt, sc_opt = _optimize_mirror(f0, opt_end, rng, quiet=True)
            f_best = f_opt
            sc_best = sc_opt
            if run_end > time.monotonic() + 4.0:
                f_pol, sc_pol = _polish_pair(f_opt, run_end, rng, quiet=True)
                if sc_pol < sc_best:
                    f_best = f_pol
                    sc_best = sc_pol
            for n in n_list:
                starts.append(_resample(f_best, n))
            done += 1

    return starts


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for the non-negative sequence minimizing 2*n*max(conv(f,f))/(sum f)^2.
    Uses coarse-to-fine optimization, mirror descent top-lag smoothing, and exact
    rank-2 coordinate polishing.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    budget_start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget_s = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_s = 2400.0

    # Robust default if budget is tiny or input is missing.
    base = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if base is None:
        base = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64)
    base = np.asarray(base, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    base = np.where(np.isfinite(base), base, 0.0)
    base = np.clip(base, 0.0, 1000.0)
    base_sum = float(base.sum())
    if base.size == 0 or base_sum <= 1e-300:
        base = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64)
        base_sum = float(base.size)
    base = base / base_sum

    if budget_s < 90.0:
        return [float(x) for x in base]

    n0 = base.size
    if n0 > 16000:
        base = _resample(base, 12000)
        n0 = base.size

    n_list = [n0]
    if n0 < 16000:
        n_long = int(round(n0 * 2.0))
        if n_long <= n0:
            n_long = n0 + max(1000, n0 // 4)
        if n_long > 16000:
            n_long = 16000
        if n_long > n0:
            n_list.append(int(n_long))

    # Unique and positive lengths.
    nn = []
    for v in n_list:
        v = int(v)
        if v > 0 and v not in nn:
            nn.append(v)
    n_list = nn if nn else [n0]

    coarse_budget = min(150.0, max(15.0, budget_s * 0.06))
    final_polish = min(500.0, max(40.0, budget_s * 0.20))
    deadline = budget_start + budget_s - 25.0
    coarse_deadline = budget_start + coarse_budget

    # Coarse-to-fine starts.
    coarse_starts = _coarse_starts(base, n_list, rng, coarse_deadline)

    # Build and score starts.
    starts = []
    base_norm = _normalize(base)
    starts.append(base_norm)
    for n in n_list:
        if n != base_norm.size:
            starts.append(_resample(base_norm, n))
    starts.extend(coarse_starts)
    for n in n_list:
        cnt = 70 if n <= 8000 else 40
        starts.extend(_starts_for_length(n, rng, cnt))

    if len(starts) > 250:
        starts = starts[:250]

    scored = []
    base_score = _score_fft(base_norm)
    scored.append((float(base_score), -1, base_norm, base_norm.size))
    idx = 0
    for f in starts[1:]:
        try:
            s = _score_fft(f)
            if np.isfinite(s):
                scored.append((float(s), idx, f, f.size))
            idx += 1
        except Exception:
            idx += 1

    if not scored:
        scored = [(float(base_score), -1, base_norm, base_norm.size)]

    scored.sort()

    best_score = scored[0][0]
    best_f = scored[0][2].copy()

    selected = []
    used_ids = set()

    # Always keep the base sequence.
    for item in scored:
        if item[1] == -1 and np.isfinite(item[0]):
            selected.append(item[2])
            used_ids.add(item[1])
            break

    # Add a few of the best overall.
    cnt_add = 0
    for item in scored:
        if cnt_add >= 3:
            break
        if item[1] not in used_ids and np.isfinite(item[0]):
            selected.append(item[2])
            used_ids.add(item[1])
            cnt_add += 1

    # Add best of each target length for diversity.
    for n in n_list:
        best_item = None
        for item in scored:
            if item[3] == n and item[1] not in used_ids and np.isfinite(item[0]):
                best_item = item
                break
        if best_item is not None and len(selected) < 8:
            selected.append(best_item[2])
            used_ids.add(best_item[1])

    if not selected:
        selected = [base_norm]

    # Main phase deadlines.
    main_end = deadline - final_polish - 10.0
    if main_end < time.monotonic() + 60.0:
        main_end = deadline - 5.0

    base_weights = (1.5, 1.0, 0.9, 0.8, 0.7, 0.6, 0.5, 0.4)
    weights = []
    for kk, f in enumerate(selected):
        w = base_weights[min(kk, len(base_weights) - 1)]
        length_factor = min(1.0, max(0.30, float(n0) / float(f.size)))
        weights.append(w * length_factor)

    for idx in range(len(selected)):
        remaining_main = main_end - time.monotonic()
        if remaining_main < 60.0:
            break
        remaining_w = 0.0
        for w in weights[idx:]:
            remaining_w += w
        if remaining_w <= 0.0:
            break
        alloc = remaining_main * (weights[idx] / remaining_w)
        start_t = time.monotonic()
        opt_end = start_t + 0.75 * alloc
        pol_end = start_t + alloc
        f_start = selected[idx]
        sc_start = _score_fft(f_start)

        try:
            f_opt, sc_opt = _optimize_mirror(f_start, opt_end, rng, quiet=(alloc < 120.0))
        except Exception:
            f_opt = f_start
            sc_opt = sc_start

        f_sel = f_start
        sc_sel = sc_start
        if np.isfinite(sc_opt) and sc_opt < sc_sel:
            f_sel = f_opt
            sc_sel = sc_opt

        if pol_end > time.monotonic() + 5.0:
            try:
                f_pol, sc_pol = _polish_pair(f_sel, pol_end, rng, quiet=(alloc < 180.0))
                if np.isfinite(sc_pol) and sc_pol < sc_sel:
                    f_sel = f_pol
                    sc_sel = sc_pol
            except Exception:
                pass

        if np.isfinite(sc_sel) and sc_sel < best_score:
            best_score = sc_sel
            best_f = f_sel.copy()
            print("  new best score=%.8f length=%d" % (best_score, best_f.size))

    # Final polish on the best sequence.
    final_deadline = deadline - 2.0
    remaining = final_deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 20.0 and best_f is not None:
        pol_deadline = min(final_deadline, time.monotonic() + min(final_polish, remaining))
        try:
            f_pol, sc_pol = _polish_pair(best_f, pol_deadline, rng, quiet=False)
            if np.isfinite(sc_pol) and sc_pol < best_score:
                best_score = sc_pol
                best_f = f_pol
                print("  final polish score=%.8f" % best_score)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Clean and validate output.
    if best_f is None or best_f.size == 0:
        best_f = base_norm
    best_f = np.asarray(best_f, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    best_f = np.where(np.isfinite(best_f), best_f, 0.0)
    best_f = np.clip(best_f, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(best_f.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        best_f = np.ones(max(1, best_f.size), dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(best_f.size)
    best_f = best_f / s

    # Optional sanity check for small lengths; we do not depend on it.
    if best_f.size <= 7000 and time.monotonic() < budget_start + budget_s - 12.0:
        try:
            check = evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in best_f])
            if not np.isfinite(check):
                best_f = base_norm
        except Exception:
            pass

    return [float(x) for x in best_f]
```
